c4:Tb17b,<p>The RegExp <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> applies the following grammar to the input pattern String.       <h2>Syntax</h2>

<emu-production id="prod-UnicodeLeadSurrogate" name="UnicodeLeadSurrogate"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-UnicodeLeadSurrogate">UnicodeLeadSurrogate</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-gprose>&gt; any Unicode code point in the inclusive interval from U+D800 to U+DBFF</emu-gprose></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-UnicodeTrailSurrogate" name="UnicodeTrailSurrogate"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-UnicodeTrailSurrogate">UnicodeTrailSurrogate</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-gprose>&gt; any Unicode code point in the inclusive interval from U+DC00 to U+DFFF</emu-gprose></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
      <p>Each <code>\\u</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexTrailSurrogate">HexTrailSurrogate</a></emu-nt> for which the choice of associated <code>u</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexLeadSurrogate">HexLeadSurrogate</a></emu-nt> is ambiguous shall be associated with the nearest possible <code>u</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexLeadSurrogate">HexLeadSurrogate</a></emu-nt> that would otherwise have no corresponding <code>\\u</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexTrailSurrogate">HexTrailSurrogate</a></emu-nt>.</p>
      <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-HexLeadSurrogate" name="HexLeadSurrogate"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexLeadSurrogate">HexLeadSurrogate</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-Hex4Digits">Hex4Digits</a></emu-nt> <emu-constraints>[> but only if the MV of |Hex4Digits| is in the inclusive interval from 0xD800 to 0xDBFF]</emu-constraints></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-HexTrailSurrogate" name="HexTrailSurrogate"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-HexTrailSurrogate">HexTrailSurrogate</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-Hex4Digits">Hex4Digits</a></emu-nt> <emu-constraints>[> but only if the MV of |Hex4Digits| is in the inclusive interval from 0xDC00 to 0xDFFF]</emu-constraints></emu-rhs></emu-production>

da:T9ef,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This method returns a single element String containing the code unit at index <var>position</var> within the String value resulting from converting this object to a String. If there is no element at that index, the result is the empty String. The result <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type">is a String</a></emu-xref> value, not a String object.</p>
          <p>If <code>pos</code> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref>, then the result of <code>x.charAt(pos)</code> is equivalent to the result of <code>x.substring(pos, pos + 1)</code>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <p>This method performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>thisValue</var> be the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value.</li><li>Perform ? <emu-xref aoid="RequireObjectCoercible"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-requireobjectcoercible">RequireObjectCoercible</a></emu-xref>(<var>thisValue</var>).</li><li>Let <var>string</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>thisValue</var>).</li><li>Set <var>position</var> to ? <emu-xref aoid="ToIntegerOrInfinity"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tointegerorinfinity">ToIntegerOrInfinity</a></emu-xref>(<var>position</var>).</li><li>Let <var>size</var> be the length of <var>string</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>position</var> &lt; 0 or <var>position</var> ≥ <var>size</var>, return the empty String.</li><li class="exit">Return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring">substring</a></emu-xref> of <var>string</var> from <var>position</var> to <var>position</var> + 1.</li></ol></emu-alg>

e2:T1d44,<p>An ECMAScript implementation that includes the ECMA-402 Internationalization API must implement this method as specified in the ECMA-402 specification. If an ECMAScript implementation does not include the ECMA-402 API the following specification of this method is used:</p>
        <p>This method returns a Number other than <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> representing the result of an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> locale-sensitive String comparison of the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value (converted to a String <var>string</var>) with <var>that</var> (converted to a String <var>thatValue</var>). The result is intended to correspond with a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/indexed-collections#sort-order"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/indexed-collections#sort-order">sort order</a></emu-xref> of String values according to conventions of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s current locale, and will be negative when <var>string</var> is ordered before <var>thatValue</var>, positive when <var>string</var> is ordered after <var>thatValue</var>, and zero in all other cases (representing no relative ordering between <var>string</var> and <var>thatValue</var>).</p>
        <p>Before performing the comparisons, this method performs the following steps to prepare the Strings:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>thisValue</var> be the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value.</li><li>Perform ? <emu-xref aoid="RequireObjectCoercible"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-requireobjectcoercible">RequireObjectCoercible</a></emu-xref>(<var>thisValue</var>).</li><li>Let <var>string</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>thisValue</var>).</li><li>Let <var>thatValue</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>that</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <p>The meaning of the optional second and third parameters to this method are defined in the ECMA-402 specification; implementations that do not include ECMA-402 support must not assign any other interpretation to those parameter positions.</p>
        <p>The actual return values are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> to permit encoding additional information in them, but this method, when considered as a method of two arguments, is required to be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/indexed-collections#consistent-comparator"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/indexed-collections#consistent-comparator">consistent comparator</a></emu-xref> defining a total ordering on the set of all Strings. This method is also required to recognize and honour canonical equivalence according to the Unicode Standard, including returning <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> when comparing distinguishable Strings that are canonically equivalent.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This method itself is not directly suitable as an argument to <code>Array.prototype.sort</code> because the latter requires a function of two arguments.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This method may rely on whatever language- and/or locale-sensitive comparison functionality is available to the ECMAScript environment from the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>, and is intended to compare according to the conventions of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host-environment">host environment</a></emu-xref>'s current locale. However, regardless of comparison capabilities, this method must recognize and honour canonical equivalence according to the Unicode Standard—for example, the following comparisons must all return <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x212B; ANGSTROM SIGN vs.</span>
            <span class="hljs-comment">// A&amp;#x030A; LATIN CAPITAL LETTER A + COMBINING RING ABOVE</span>
            <span class="hljs-string">&quot;\u212B&quot;</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">localeCompare</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;A\u030A&quot;</span>)

            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x2126; OHM SIGN vs.</span>
            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x03A9; GREEK CAPITAL LETTER OMEGA</span>
            <span class="hljs-string">&quot;\u2126&quot;</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">localeCompare</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;\u03A9&quot;</span>)

            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x1E69; LATIN SMALL LETTER S WITH DOT BELOW AND DOT ABOVE vs.</span>
            <span class="hljs-comment">// s&amp;#x0307;&amp;#x0323; LATIN SMALL LETTER S + COMBINING DOT ABOVE + COMBINING DOT BELOW</span>
            <span class="hljs-string">&quot;\u1E69&quot;</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">localeCompare</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;s\u0307\u0323&quot;</span>)

            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x1E0B;&amp;#x0323; LATIN SMALL LETTER D WITH DOT ABOVE + COMBINING DOT BELOW vs.</span>
            <span class="hljs-comment">// &amp;#x1E0D;&amp;#x0307; LATIN SMALL LETTER D WITH DOT BELOW + COMBINING DOT ABOVE</span>

        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The value of <var>separator</var> may be an empty String. In this case, <var>separator</var> does not match the empty <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> at the beginning or end of the input String, nor does it match the empty <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> at the end of the previous separator match. If <var>separator</var> is the empty String, the String is split up into individual code unit elements; the length of the result array equals the length of the String, and each <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> contains one code unit.</p>
          <p>If the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value is (or converts to) the empty String, the result depends on whether <var>separator</var> can match the empty String. If it can, the result array contains no elements. Otherwise, the result array contains one element, which is the empty String.</p>
          <p>If <var>separator</var> is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then the result array contains just one String, which is the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value (converted to a String). If <var>limit</var> is not <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then the output array is truncated so that it contains no more than <var>limit</var> elements.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <ul>
          <li>

105:T1635,<p>A regular expression pattern is converted into an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> using the process described below. An implementation is encouraged to use more efficient algorithms than the ones listed below, as long as the results are the same. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> is used as the value of a RegExp object's <var class="field">[[RegExpMatcher]]</var> internal slot.</p>
      <p>A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a></emu-nt> is a BMP pattern if its associated flags contain neither a <code>u</code> nor a <code>v</code>. Otherwise, it is a Unicode pattern. A BMP pattern matches against a String interpreted as consisting of a sequence of 16-bit values that are Unicode code points in the range of the Basic Multilingual Plane. A Unicode pattern matches against a String interpreted as consisting of Unicode code points encoded using UTF-16. In the context of describing the behaviour of a BMP pattern “character” means a single 16-bit Unicode BMP code point. In the context of describing the behaviour of a Unicode pattern “character” means a UTF-16 encoded code point (<emu-xref href="#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type">6.1.4</a></emu-xref>). In either context, “character value” means the numeric value of the corresponding non-encoded code point.</p>
      <p>The syntax and semantics of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a></emu-nt> is defined as if the source text for the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a></emu-nt> was a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#prod-SourceCharacter">SourceCharacter</a></emu-nt> values where each <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#prod-SourceCharacter">SourceCharacter</a></emu-nt> corresponds to a Unicode code point. If a BMP pattern contains a non-BMP <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#prod-SourceCharacter">SourceCharacter</a></emu-nt> the entire pattern is encoded using UTF-16 and the individual code units of that encoding are used as the elements of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>For example, consider a pattern expressed in source text as the single non-BMP character U+1D11E (MUSICAL SYMBOL G CLEF). Interpreted as a Unicode pattern, it would be a single element (character) <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> consisting of the single code point U+1D11E. However, interpreted as a BMP pattern, it is first UTF-16 encoded to produce a two element <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> consisting of the code units 0xD834 and 0xDD1E.</p>
        <p>Patterns are passed to the RegExp <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> as ECMAScript String values in which non-BMP characters are UTF-16 encoded. For example, the single character MUSICAL SYMBOL G CLEF pattern, expressed as a String value, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type">is a String</a></emu-xref> of length 2 whose elements were the code units 0xD834 and 0xDD1E. So no further translation of the string would be necessary to process it as a BMP pattern consisting of two pattern characters. However, to process it as a Unicode pattern <emu-xref aoid="UTF16SurrogatePairToCodePoint"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-utf16decodesurrogatepair">UTF16SurrogatePairToCodePoint</a></emu-xref> must be used in producing a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> whose sole element is a single pattern character, the code point U+1D11E.</p>
        <p>An implementation may not actually perform such translations to or from UTF-16, but the semantics of this specification requires that the result of pattern matching be as if such translations were performed.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

12c:Ta6a,<p>The abstract operation RegExpExec takes arguments <var>regexp</var> (an Object) and <var>string</var> (a String) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> either an Object or <emu-val>null</emu-val>, or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>.         <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>exec</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="Get"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-get-o-p">Get</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexp</var>, <emu-val>"exec"</emu-val>).</li><li>If <emu-xref aoid="IsCallable"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-iscallable">IsCallable</a></emu-xref>(<var>exec</var>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>result</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="Call"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-call">Call</a></emu-xref>(<var>exec</var>, <var>regexp</var>, « <var>string</var> »).</li><li class="exit">If <var>result</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is not an Object</a></emu-xref> and <var>result</var> is not <emu-val>null</emu-val>, throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>result</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Perform ? <emu-xref aoid="RequireInternalSlot"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-requireinternalslot">RequireInternalSlot</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexp</var>, <var class="field">[[RegExpMatcher]]</var>).</li><li class="exit">Return ? <emu-xref aoid="RegExpBuiltinExec"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexpbuiltinexec">RegExpBuiltinExec</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexp</var>, <var>string</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>If a callable <emu-val>"exec"</emu-val> property is not found this algorithm falls back to attempting to use the built-in RegExp matching algorithm. This provides compatible behaviour for code written for prior editions where most built-in algorithms that use regular expressions did not perform a dynamic property lookup of <emu-val>"exec"</emu-val>.</p>

134:T7bc,<p>RegExp instances are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary objects</a></emu-xref> that inherit properties from the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-properties-of-the-regexp-prototype-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-properties-of-the-regexp-prototype-object">RegExp prototype object</a></emu-xref>. RegExp instances have internal slots <var class="field">[[OriginalSource]]</var>, <var class="field">[[OriginalFlags]]</var>, <var class="field">[[RegExpRecord]]</var>, and <var class="field">[[RegExpMatcher]]</var>. The value of the <var class="field">[[RegExpMatcher]]</var> internal slot is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> representation of the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a></emu-nt> of the RegExp object.</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Prior to ECMAScript 2015, RegExp instances were specified as having the own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data properties</a></emu-xref> <emu-val>"source"</emu-val>, <emu-val>"global"</emu-val>, <emu-val>"ignoreCase"</emu-val>, and <emu-val>"multiline"</emu-val>.       </div></emu-note>

from 0 to 1.</li><li>Let <var>refReplacement</var> be <var>ref</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>refLength</var> be the length of <var>ref</var>.</li><li>Set <var>templateRemainder</var> to the 13c:T2ad8,<p>The descriptions below use the following internal data structures:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>
            A <dfn>CharSetElement</dfn> is one of the two following entities:
            <ul>
              <li>
                If <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[UnicodeSets]]</var> is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, then a CharSetElement is a character in the sense of the Pattern Semantics above.
              </li>
              <li>
                If <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[UnicodeSets]]</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then a CharSetElement is a sequence whose elements are characters in the sense of the Pattern Semantics above. This includes the empty sequence, sequences of one character, and sequences of more than one character. For convenience, when working with CharSetElements of this kind, an individual character is treated interchangeably with a sequence of one character.
              </li>
            </ul>
          </li>
          <li>
            A <dfn id="pattern-charset" variants="CharSets">CharSet</dfn> is a mathematical set of CharSetElements.
          </li>
          <li>
            A <dfn id="pattern-capturerange" variants="CaptureRanges">CaptureRange</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> { <var class="field">[[StartIndex]]</var>, <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var> } that represents the range of characters included in a capture, where <var class="field">[[StartIndex]]</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> representing the start index (inclusive) of the range within <var>input</var>, and <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> representing the end index (exclusive) of the range within <var>input</var>. For any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange">CaptureRange</a></emu-xref>, these indices must satisfy the invariant that <var class="field">[[StartIndex]]</var> ≤ <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var>.
          </li>
          <li>
            A <dfn id="pattern-matchstate" variants="MatchStates" oldids="pattern-matchresult">MatchState</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> { <var class="field">[[Input]]</var>, <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var>, <var class="field">[[Captures]]</var> } where <var class="field">[[Input]]</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of characters representing the String being matched, <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>, and <var class="field">[[Captures]]</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of values, one for each <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-countleftcapturingparenswithin"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-countleftcapturingparenswithin">left-capturing parenthesis</a></emu-xref> in the pattern. <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchStates</a></emu-xref> are used to represent partial match states in the regular expression matching algorithms. The <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var> is one plus the index of the last input character matched so far by the pattern, while <var class="field">[[Captures]]</var> holds the results of capturing parentheses. The <var>n</var><sup>th</sup> element of <var class="field">[[Captures]]</var> is either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange">CaptureRange</a></emu-xref> representing the range of characters captured by the <var>n</var><sup>th</sup> set of capturing parentheses, or <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> if the <var>n</var><sup>th</sup> set of capturing parentheses hasn't been reached yet. Due to backtracking, many <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchStates</a></emu-xref> may be in use at any time during the matching process.
          </li>
          <li>
            A <dfn id="pattern-matchercontinuation" variants="MatcherContinuations">MatcherContinuation</dfn> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> that takes one <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> argument and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>failure</emu-const>. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> attempts to match the remaining portion (specified by the closure's captured values) of the pattern against <var>input</var>, starting at the intermediate state given by its <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> argument. If the match succeeds, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> returns the final <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> that it reached; if the match fails, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> returns <emu-const>failure</emu-const>.
          </li>
          <li>
            A <dfn id="pattern-matcher" variants="Matchers">Matcher</dfn> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> that takes two arguments—a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> and a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref>—and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>failure</emu-const>. A <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref> attempts to match a middle subpattern (specified by the closure's captured values) of the pattern against the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref>'s <var class="field">[[Input]]</var>, starting at the intermediate state given by its <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> argument. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> argument should be a closure that matches the rest of the pattern. After matching the subpattern of a pattern to obtain a new <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref>, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref> then calls <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> on that new <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> to test if the rest of the pattern can match as well. If it can, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref> returns the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> returned by <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref>; if not, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref> may try different choices at its choice points, repeatedly calling <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref> until it either succeeds or all possibilities have been exhausted.
          </li>
        </ul>

        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>A Pattern compiles to an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> value. <emu-xref aoid="RegExpBuiltinExec"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexpbuiltinexec">RegExpBuiltinExec</a></emu-xref> can then apply this procedure to a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of characters and an offset within that <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> to determine whether the pattern would match starting at exactly that offset within the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>, and, if it does match, what the values of the capturing parentheses would be. The algorithms in <emu-xref href="#sec-pattern-semantics"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-pattern-semantics">22.2.2</a></emu-xref> are designed so that compiling a pattern may throw a <emu-val>SyntaxError</emu-val> exception; on the other hand, once the pattern is successfully compiled, applying the resulting <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> to find a match in a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of characters cannot throw an exception (except for any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> exceptions that can occur anywhere such as out-of-memory).</p>

        <!-- Disjunction -->
        <p>It is defined piecewise over the following productions:</p>
<emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-Disjunction" name="Disjunction" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> <emu-t>|</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>m1</var> be CompileSubpattern of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> with arguments <var>regexpRecord</var> and <var>direction</var>.</li><li>Let <var>m2</var> be CompileSubpattern of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> with arguments <var>regexpRecord</var> and <var>direction</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="MatchTwoAlternatives"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-matchtwoalternatives">MatchTwoAlternatives</a></emu-xref>(<var>m1</var>, <var>m2</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The <code>|</code> regular expression operator separates two alternatives. The pattern first tries to match the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> (followed by the sequel of the regular expression); if it fails, it tries to match the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> (followed by the sequel of the regular expression). If the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt>, the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt>, and the sequel all have choice points, all choices in the sequel are tried before moving on to the next choice in the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt>. If choices in the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> are exhausted, the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> is tried instead of the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt>. Any capturing parentheses inside a portion of the pattern skipped by <code>|</code> produce <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> values instead of Strings. Thus, for example,</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/a|ab/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abc&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
          <p>returns the result <emu-val>"a"</emu-val> and not <emu-val>"ab"</emu-val>. Moreover,</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/((a)|(ab))((c)|(bc))/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abc&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
          <p>returns the array</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abc&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;bc&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;bc&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
          <p>and not</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abc&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ab&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ab&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;c&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;c&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>]</code></pre>
          <p>The order in which the two alternatives are tried is independent of the value of <var>direction</var>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <!-- Alternative -->
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-Alternative" name="Alternative" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-constraints>[empty]</emu-constraints></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="EmptyMatcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-emptymatcher">EmptyMatcher</a></emu-xref>().</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-Alternative" name="Alternative" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>m1</var> be CompileSubpattern of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> with arguments <var>regexpRecord</var> and <var>direction</var>.</li><li>Let <var>m2</var> be CompileSubpattern of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt> with arguments <var>regexpRecord</var> and <var>direction</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="MatchSequence"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-matchsequence">MatchSequence</a></emu-xref>(<var>m1</var>, <var>m2</var>, <var>direction</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Consecutive <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt>s try to simultaneously match consecutive portions of <var>input</var>. When <var>direction</var> is <emu-const>forward</emu-const>, if the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt>, the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt>, and the sequel of the regular expression all have choice points, all choices in the sequel are tried before moving on to the next choice in the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt>, and all choices in the right <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt> are tried before moving on to the next choice in the left <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt>. When <var>direction</var> is <emu-const>backward</emu-const>, the evaluation order of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Alternative">Alternative</a></emu-nt> and <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Term">Term</a></emu-nt> are reversed.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

144:T6d8a,<p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-syntax-directed-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-syntax-directed-operations">syntax-directed operation</a></emu-xref> CompileAssertion takes argument <var>regexpRecord</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records">RegExp Record</a></emu-xref>) and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This section is amended in <emu-xref href="#sec-compileassertion-annexb"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#sec-compileassertion-annexb">B.1.2.6</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <p>It is defined piecewise over the following productions:</p>
<emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-Assertion" name="Assertion" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Assertion">Assertion</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-t>^</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return a new <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref> with parameters (<var>matchState</var>, <var>continue</var>) that captures <var>regexpRecord</var> and performs the following steps when called:<ol><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>matchState</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>continue</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref>.</li><li>Let <var>input</var> be <var>matchState</var>.<var class="field">[[Input]]</var>.</li><li>Let <var>e</var> be <var>matchState</var>.<var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var>.</li><li>If <var>e</var> = 0, or if <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[Multiline]]</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val> and the character <var>input</var>[<var>e</var> - 1] is matched by <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-LineTerminator">LineTerminator</a></emu-nt>, then<ol><li class="exit">Return <var>continue</var>(<var>matchState</var>).</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <emu-const>failure</emu-const>.</li></ol></li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Even when the <code>y</code> flag is used with a pattern, <code>^</code> always matches only at the beginning of <var>input</var>, or (if <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[Multiline]]</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val>) at the beginning of a line.</p>

        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The form <code>(?=</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <code>)</code> specifies a zero-width positive lookahead. In order for it to succeed, the pattern inside <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> must match at the current position, but the current position is not advanced before matching the sequel. If <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> can match at the current position in several ways, only the first one is tried. Unlike other regular expression operators, there is no backtracking into a <code>(?=</code> form (this unusual behaviour is inherited from Perl). This only matters when the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> contains capturing parentheses and the sequel of the pattern contains backreferences to those captures.</p>
          <p>For example,</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(?=(a+))/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;baaabac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
          <p>matches the empty String immediately after the first <code>b</code> and therefore returns the array:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;aaa&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
          <p>To illustrate the lack of backtracking into the lookahead, consider:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(?=(a+))a*b\<span class="hljs-number">1</span>/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;baaabac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
          <p>This expression returns</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aba&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
          <p>and not:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aaaba&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-Assertion" name="Assertion" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-Assertion">Assertion</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-t>(?!</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <emu-t>)</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>

        <emu-note data-num="4"><span class="note">Note 4</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The form <code>(?!</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <code>)</code> specifies a zero-width negative lookahead. In order for it to succeed, the pattern inside <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> must fail to match at the current position. The current position is not advanced before matching the sequel. <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> can contain capturing parentheses, but backreferences to them only make sense from within <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> itself. Backreferences to these capturing parentheses from elsewhere in the pattern always return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> because the negative lookahead must fail for the pattern to succeed. For example,</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(.*?)<span class="hljs-title function_">a</span>(?!(a+)b\2c)\<span class="hljs-number">2</span>(.*)/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;baaabaac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
          <p>looks for an <code>a</code> not immediately followed by some positive number n of <code>a</code>'s, a <code>b</code>, another n <code>a</code>'s (specified by the first <code>\\2</code>) and a <code>c</code>. The second <code>\\2</code> is outside the negative lookahead, so it matches against <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> and therefore always succeeds. The whole expression returns the array:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;baaabaac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ba&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;abaac&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
        </div></emu-note>

href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>ye</var> ≤ <var>xe</var>.</li><li>Let <var>r</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-capturerange">CaptureRange</a></emu-xref> { <var class="field">[[StartIndex]]</var>: <var>ye</var>, <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var>: <var>xe</var> }.</li></ol></li><li>Set <var>capability</var>[<var>parenIndex</var> + 1] to <var>r</var>.</li><li>Let <var>z</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> { <var class="field">[[Input]]</var>: <var>input</var>, <var class="field">[[EndIndex]]</var>: <var>ye</var>, <var class="field">[[Captures]]</var>: <var>capability</var> }.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>c</var>(<var>z</var>).</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <var>m</var>(<var>x</var>, <var>d</var>).</li></ol></li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Parentheses of the form <code>(</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <code>)</code> serve both to group the components of the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> pattern together and to save the result of the match. The result can be used either in a backreference (<code>\\</code> followed by a non-zero decimal number), referenced in a replace String, or returned as part of an array from the regular expression matching <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref>. To inhibit the capturing behaviour of parentheses, use the form <code>(?:</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Disjunction">Disjunction</a></emu-nt> <code>)</code> instead.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>An escape sequence of the form <code>\\</code> followed by a non-zero decimal number <var>n</var> matches the result of the <var>n</var><sup>th</sup> set of capturing parentheses (<emu-xref href="#sec-pattern-notation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-pattern-notation">22.2.2.1</a></emu-xref>). It is an error if the regular expression has fewer than <var>n</var> capturing parentheses. If the regular expression has <var>n</var> or more capturing parentheses but the <var>n</var><sup>th</sup> one is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> because it has not captured anything, then the backreference always succeeds.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

14e:Tb5e3,<p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-syntax-directed-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-syntax-directed-operations">syntax-directed operation</a></emu-xref> CompileToCharSet takes argument <var>regexpRecord</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records">RegExp Record</a></emu-xref>) and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset">CharSet</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This section is amended in <emu-xref href="#sec-compiletocharset-annexb"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#sec-compiletocharset-annexb">B.1.2.8</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <!-- ClassContents -->
        <p>It is defined piecewise over the following productions:</p>
<emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ClassContents" name="ClassContents" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassContents">ClassContents</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-constraints>[empty]</emu-constraints></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return the empty <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset">CharSet</a></emu-xref>.</li></ol></emu-alg>

        <!-- NonemptyClassRangesNoDash -->

        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassContents">ClassContents</a></emu-nt> can expand into a single <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> and/or ranges of two <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> separated by dashes. In the latter case the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassContents">ClassContents</a></emu-nt> includes all characters between the first <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> and the second <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt>, inclusive; an error occurs if either <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> does not represent a single character (for example, if one is \w) or if the first <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt>'s character value is strictly greater than the second <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt>'s character value.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Even if the pattern ignores case, the case of the two ends of a range is significant in determining which characters belong to the range. Thus, for example, the pattern <code>/[E-F]/i</code> matches only the letters <code>E</code>, <code>F</code>, <code>e</code>, and <code>f</code>, while the pattern <code>/[E-f]/i</code> matches all uppercase and lowercase letters in the Unicode Basic Latin block as well as the symbols <code>[</code>, <code>\\</code>, <code>]</code>, <code>^</code>, <code>_</code>, and <code>`</code>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="4"><span class="note">Note 4</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>A <code>-</code> character can be treated literally or it can denote a range. It is treated literally if it is the first or last character of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassContents">ClassContents</a></emu-nt>, the beginning or end limit of a range specification, or immediately follows a range specification.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

        <!-- ClassAtom -->
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ClassAtom" name="ClassAtom" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-t>-</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset">CharSet</a></emu-xref> containing the single character <code>-</code> U+002D (HYPHEN-MINUS).</li></ol></emu-alg>

        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ClassAtomNoDash" name="ClassAtomNoDash" collapsed=""><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-ClassAtomNoDash">ClassAtomNoDash</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq> <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#prod-SourceCharacter">SourceCharacter</a></emu-nt> but not <emu-oneof>one of</emu-oneof> <emu-t>\</emu-t> or <emu-t>]</emu-t> or <emu-t>-</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>

        <!-- ClassEscape -->
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ClassEscape" name="ClassEscape"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-ClassEscape">ClassEscape</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>b</emu-t></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>-</emu-t></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-CharacterEscape">CharacterEscape</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>charValue</var> be the <emu-xref aoid="CharacterValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-patterns-static-semantics-character-value">CharacterValue</a></emu-xref> of this <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#prod-ClassEscape">ClassEscape</a></emu-nt>.</li><li>Let <var>char</var> be the character whose character value is <var>charValue</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-charset">CharSet</a></emu-xref> containing the single character <var>char</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="5"><span class="note">Note 5</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> can use any of the escape sequences that are allowed in the rest of the regular expression except for <code>\\b</code>, <code>\\B</code>, and backreferences. Inside a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-CharacterClass">CharacterClass</a></emu-nt>, <code>\\b</code> means the backspace character, while <code>\\B</code> and backreferences raise errors. Using a backreference inside a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-ClassAtom">ClassAtom</a></emu-nt> causes an error.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

          <p>The result will often consist of two or more ranges.         </div></emu-note>

        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>regexp</var> be the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value.</li><li class="exit">If <var>regexp</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is not an Object</a></emu-xref>, throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li>If <var>regexp</var> does not have an <var class="field">[[OriginalSource]]</var> internal slot, then<ol><li class="exit">If <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexp</var>, <emu-intrinsic>%RegExp.prototype%</emu-intrinsic>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, return <emu-val>"(?:)"</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">Throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception.</li></ol></li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>regexp</var> has an <var class="field">[[OriginalFlags]]</var> internal slot.</li><li>Let <var>source</var> be <var>regexp</var>.<var class="field">[[OriginalSource]]</var>.</li><li>Let <var>flags</var> be <var>regexp</var>.<var class="field">[[OriginalFlags]]</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="EscapeRegExpPattern"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-escaperegexppattern">EscapeRegExpPattern</a></emu-xref>(<var>source</var>, <var>flags</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>

        163:T10ea,<p>The abstract operation EscapeRegExpPattern takes arguments <var>pattern</var> (a String) and <var>flags</var> (a String) and returns a String. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li>If <var>flags</var> contains <emu-val>"v"</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>patternSymbol</var> be <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[+UnicodeMode, +UnicodeSetsMode]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt>.</li></ol></li><li>Else if <var>flags</var> contains <emu-val>"u"</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>patternSymbol</var> be <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[+UnicodeMode, ~UnicodeSetsMode]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>Let <var>patternSymbol</var> be <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Pattern">Pattern</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[<emu-const>UnicodeMode, </emu-const>UnicodeSetsMode]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>escapedPattern</var> be a String in the form of a <var>patternSymbol</var> equivalent to <var>pattern</var> interpreted as UTF-16 encoded Unicode code points (<emu-xref href="#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type">6.1.4</a></emu-xref>), in which certain code points are escaped as described below. <var>escapedPattern</var> may or may not differ from <var>pattern</var>; however, the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> that would result from evaluating <var>escapedPattern</var> as a <var>patternSymbol</var> must behave identically to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref> given by the constructed object's <var class="field">[[RegExpMatcher]]</var> internal slot. Multiple calls to this abstract operation using the same values for <var>pattern</var> and <var>flags</var> must produce identical results.</li><li>The code points <code>/</code> or any <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-LineTerminator">LineTerminator</a></emu-nt> occurring in the pattern shall be escaped in <var>escapedPattern</var> as necessary to ensure that the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <emu-val>"/"</emu-val>, <var>escapedPattern</var>, <emu-val>"/"</emu-val>, and <var>flags</var> can be parsed (in an appropriate lexical context) as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-RegularExpressionLiteral">RegularExpressionLiteral</a></emu-nt> that behaves identically to the constructed regular expression. For example, if <var>pattern</var> is <emu-val>"/"</emu-val>, then <var>escapedPattern</var> could be <emu-val>"\\/"</emu-val> or <emu-val>"\\u002F"</emu-val>, among other possibilities, but not <emu-val>"/"</emu-val>, because <code>///</code> followed by <var>flags</var> would be parsed as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-SingleLineComment">SingleLineComment</a></emu-nt> rather than a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-RegularExpressionLiteral">RegularExpressionLiteral</a></emu-nt>. If <var>pattern</var> is the empty String, this specification can be met by letting <var>escapedPattern</var> be <emu-val>"(?:)"</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>escapedPattern</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>

          <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>Despite having similar names, <code>RegExp.escape</code> and EscapeRegExpPattern do not perform similar actions. The former escapes a string for representation inside a pattern, while this function escapes a pattern for representation as a string.</p>

164:T316e,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This method returns an Array into which substrings of the result of converting <var>string</var> to a String have been stored. The substrings are determined by searching from left to right for matches of the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value regular expression; these occurrences are not part of any String in the returned array, but serve to divide up the String value.</p>
          <p>The <emu-val>this</emu-val> value may be an empty regular expression or a regular expression that can match an empty String. In this case, the regular expression does not match the empty <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> at the beginning or end of the input String, nor does it match the empty <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> at the end of the previous separator match. (For example, if the regular expression matches the empty String, the String is split up into individual code unit elements; the length of the result array equals the length of the String, and each <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> contains one code unit.) Only the first match at a given index of the String is considered, even if backtracking could yield a non-empty <emu-not-ref>substring</emu-not-ref> match at that index. (For example, <code>/a*?/[Symbol.split]("ab")</code> evaluates to the array <code>["a", "b"]</code>, while <code>/a*/[Symbol.split]("ab")</code> evaluates to the array <code>["","b"]</code>.)</p>
          <p>If <var>string</var> is (or converts to) the empty String, the result depends on whether the regular expression can match the empty String. If it can, the result array contains no elements. Otherwise, the result array contains one element, which is the empty String.</p>
          <p>If the regular expression contains capturing parentheses, then each time <var>separator</var> is matched the results (including any <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> results) of the capturing parentheses are spliced into the output array. For example,</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/&lt;(\/)?([^<span class="language-xml"><span class="hljs-tag">&lt;&gt;</span>]+)&gt;/[Symbol.split](&quot;A<span class="hljs-tag">&lt;<span class="hljs-name">B</span>&gt;</span>bold<span class="hljs-tag">&lt;/<span class="hljs-name">B</span>&gt;</span>and<span class="hljs-tag">&lt;<span class="hljs-name">CODE</span>&gt;</span>coded<span class="hljs-tag">&lt;/<span class="hljs-name">CODE</span>&gt;</span>&quot;)</span></code></pre>
          <p>evaluates to the array</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;A&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;B&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;bold&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;/&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;B&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;and&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;CODE&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;coded&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;/&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;CODE&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
          <p>If <var>limit</var> is not <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then the output array is truncated so that it contains no more than <var>limit</var> elements.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

170:T32a8,<p>The abstract operation RepeatMatcher takes arguments <var>m</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matcher">Matcher</a></emu-xref>), <var>min</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>), <var>max</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> or +&infin;), <var>greedy</var> (a Boolean), <var>matchState</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref>), <var>continue</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchercontinuation">MatcherContinuation</a></emu-xref>), <var>parenIndex</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>), and <var>parenCount</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#pattern-matchstate">MatchState</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>failure</emu-const>.
