          <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>An <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> followed by a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Quantifier">Quantifier</a></emu-nt> is repeated the number of times specified by the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Quantifier">Quantifier</a></emu-nt>. A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Quantifier">Quantifier</a></emu-nt> can be non-greedy, in which case the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> pattern is repeated as few times as possible while still matching the sequel, or it can be greedy, in which case the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> pattern is repeated as many times as possible while still matching the sequel. The <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> pattern is repeated rather than the input character sequence that it matches, so different repetitions of the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> can match different input substrings.</p>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>If the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> and the sequel of the regular expression all have choice points, the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> is first matched as many (or as few, if non-greedy) times as possible. All choices in the sequel are tried before moving on to the next choice in the last repetition of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt>. All choices in the last (n<sup>th</sup>) repetition of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> are tried before moving on to the next choice in the next-to-last (n - 1)<sup>st</sup> repetition of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt>; at which point it may turn out that more or fewer repetitions of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> are now possible; these are exhausted (again, starting with either as few or as many as possible) before moving on to the next choice in the (n - 1)<sup>st</sup> repetition of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> and so on.</p>
            <p>Compare</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/a[a-z]{<span class="hljs-number">2</span>,<span class="hljs-number">4</span>}/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abcdefghi&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which returns <emu-val>"abcde"</emu-val> with</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/a[a-z]{<span class="hljs-number">2</span>,<span class="hljs-number">4</span>}?/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;abcdefghi&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which returns <emu-val>"abc"</emu-val>.</p>
            <p>Consider also</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(aa|aabaac|ba|b|c)*/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aabaac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which, by the choice point ordering above, returns the array</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aaba&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ba&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
            <p>and not any of:</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
              [<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aabaac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;aabaac&quot;</span>]
              [<span class="hljs-string">&quot;aabaac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;c&quot;</span>]
            </code></pre>
            <p>The above ordering of choice points can be used to write a regular expression that calculates the greatest common divisor of two numbers (represented in unary notation). The following example calculates the gcd of 10 and 15:</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript"><span class="hljs-string">&quot;aaaaaaaaaa,aaaaaaaaaaaaaaa&quot;</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">replace</span>(<span class="hljs-regexp">/^(a+)\1*,\1+$/</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;$1&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which returns the gcd in unary notation <emu-val>"aaaaa"</emu-val>.</p>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>Step <emu-xref href="#step-repeatmatcher-clear-captures"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#step-repeatmatcher-clear-captures">4</a></emu-xref> of the RepeatMatcher clears <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt>'s captures each time <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> is repeated. We can see its behaviour in the regular expression</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(z)((a+)?(b+)?(c))*/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;zaacbbbcac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which returns the array</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;zaacbbbcac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;z&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-literal">undefined</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;c&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
            <p>and not</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;zaacbbbcac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;z&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;ac&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;a&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;bbb&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;c&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
            <p>because each iteration of the outermost <code>*</code> clears all captured Strings contained in the quantified <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt>, which in this case includes capture Strings numbered 2, 3, 4, and 5.</p>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="4"><span class="note">Note 4</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>Step <emu-xref href="#step-repeatmatcher-done"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#step-repeatmatcher-done">2.b</a></emu-xref> of the RepeatMatcher states that once the minimum number of repetitions has been satisfied, any more expansions of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#prod-Atom">Atom</a></emu-nt> that match the empty character sequence are not considered for further repetitions. This prevents the regular expression engine from falling into an infinite loop on patterns such as:</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(a*)*/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;b&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>or the slightly more complicated:</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">/(a*)b\<span class="hljs-number">1</span>+/.<span class="hljs-title function_">exec</span>(<span class="hljs-string">&quot;baaaac&quot;</span>)</code></pre>
            <p>which returns the array</p>
            <pre><code class="hljs javascript">[<span class="hljs-string">&quot;b&quot;</span>, <span class="hljs-string">&quot;&quot;</span>]</code></pre>
          </div></emu-note>

177:T1139,<p>The abstract operation Canonicalize takes arguments <var>regexpRecord</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-regexp-records">RegExp Record</a></emu-xref>) and <var>char</var> (a character) and returns a character.           <emu-alg><ol><li>If <emu-xref aoid="HasEitherUnicodeFlag"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-runtime-semantics-haseitherunicodeflag-abstract-operation">HasEitherUnicodeFlag</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexpRecord</var>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val> and <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[IgnoreCase]]</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then<ol><li class="exit">If the file <a href="https://unicode.org/Public/UCD/latest/ucd/CaseFolding.txt"><code>CaseFolding.txt</code></a> of the Unicode Character Database provides a simple or common case folding mapping for <var>char</var>, return the result of applying that mapping to <var>char</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>char</var>.</li></ol></li><li class="exit">If <var>regexpRecord</var>.<var class="field">[[IgnoreCase]]</var> is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, return <var>char</var>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>char</var> is a UTF-16 code unit.</li><li>Let <var>codePoint</var> be the code point whose numeric value is the numeric value of <var>char</var>.</li><li>Let <var>u</var> be toUppercase(« <var>codePoint</var> »), according to the Unicode Default Case Conversion algorithm.</li><li>Let <var>uString</var> be <emu-xref aoid="CodePointsToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-codepointstostring">CodePointsToString</a></emu-xref>(<var>u</var>).</li><li class="exit">If the length of <var>uString</var> ≠ 1, return <var>char</var>.</li><li>Let <var>codeUnit</var> be <var>uString</var>'s single code unit element.</li><li class="exit">If the numeric value of <var>char</var> ≥ 128 and the numeric value of <var>codeUnit</var> &lt; 128, return <var>char</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>codeUnit</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
          <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>In case-insignificant matches when <emu-xref aoid="HasEitherUnicodeFlag"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-runtime-semantics-haseitherunicodeflag-abstract-operation">HasEitherUnicodeFlag</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexpRecord</var>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, all characters are implicitly case-folded using the simple mapping provided by the Unicode Standard immediately before they are compared. The simple mapping always maps to a single code point, so it does not map, for example, <code>ß</code> (U+00DF LATIN SMALL LETTER SHARP S) to <code>ss</code> or <code>SS</code>. It may however map code points outside the Basic Latin block to code points within it—for example, <code>ſ</code> (U+017F LATIN SMALL LETTER LONG S) case-folds to <code>s</code> (U+0073 LATIN SMALL LETTER S) and <code>K</code> (U+212A KELVIN SIGN) case-folds to <code>k</code> (U+006B LATIN SMALL LETTER K). Strings containing those code points are matched by regular expressions such as <code>/[a-z]/ui</code>.</p>
            <p>In case-insignificant matches when <emu-xref aoid="HasEitherUnicodeFlag"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-runtime-semantics-haseitherunicodeflag-abstract-operation">HasEitherUnicodeFlag</a></emu-xref>(<var>regexpRecord</var>) is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, the mapping is based on Unicode Default Case Conversion algorithm toUppercase rather than toCasefold, which results in some subtle differences. For example, <code>Ω</code> (U+2126 OHM SIGN) is mapped by toUppercase to itself but by toCasefold to <code>ω</code> (U+03C9 GREEK SMALL LETTER OMEGA) along with <code>Ω</code> (U+03A9 GREEK CAPITAL LETTER OMEGA), so <emu-val>"\u2126"</emu-val> is matched by <code>/[ω]/ui</code> and <code>/[\u03A9]/ui</code> but not by <code>/[ω]/i</code> or <code>/[\u03A9]/i</code>. Also, no code point outside the Basic Latin block is mapped to a code point within it, so strings such as <emu-val>"\u017F ſ"</emu-val> and <emu-val>"\u212A K"</emu-val> are not matched by <code>/[a-z]/i</code>.</p>
          </div></emu-note>

To ensure interoperability, implementations must not support any other <emu-not-ref>property names</emu-not-ref> or aliases.</p>
          <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">

          </div></emu-note>
